  ⎿  Added 102 lines, removed 1 line
        1 +#pragma once
        2
        3 +#include <iosfwd>
        4 +#include <stdexcept>
        5
        6 +// MPint: a drop-in replacement for signed long int.
        7 +// All arithmetic, comparison, bitwise, increment/decrement, and stream
        8 +// operators are provided so legacy code needs no textual changes.
        9 +// The private mVal member will later be swapped out for a GMP/PARI/...
       10 +// multi-precision type without touching any call sites.
       11
       12  class MPint {
       13  public:
       14 +    // --- construction -------------------------------------------------------
       15 +    MPint() noexcept : mVal(0) {}
       16
       17 +    // Accept any built-in integral type without narrowing warnings.
       18 +    MPint(signed long int   v) noexcept : mVal(v) {}
       19 +    MPint(signed int        v) noexcept : mVal(v) {}
       20 +    MPint(signed short      v) noexcept : mVal(v) {}
       21 +    MPint(signed char       v) noexcept : mVal(v) {}
       22 +    MPint(unsigned long int v) noexcept : mVal(static_cast<signed long int>(v)) {}
       23 +    MPint(unsigned int      v) noexcept : mVal(static_cast<signed long int>(v)) {}
       24 +    MPint(unsigned short    v) noexcept : mVal(static_cast<signed long int>(v)) {}
       25 +    MPint(unsigned char     v) noexcept : mVal(static_cast<signed long int>(v)) {}
       26 +    MPint(long long         v) noexcept : mVal(static_cast<signed long int>(v)) {}
       27 +
       28 +    // Copy / move (both trivial for a scalar wrapper)
       29 +    MPint(const MPint&)            = default;
       30 +    MPint& operator=(const MPint&) = default;
       31 +
       32 +    // --- conversion out ------------------------------------------------------
       33 +    // explicit so code like "if (n)" still works (contextual bool conversion)
       34 +    // but silent implicit truncation to int, etc., is prevented.
       35 +    explicit operator signed long int()   const noexcept { return mVal; }
       36 +    explicit operator signed int()        const noexcept { return static_cast<int>(mVal); }
       37 +    explicit operator signed short()      const noexcept { return static_cast<short>(mVal); }
       38 +    explicit operator signed char()       const noexcept { return static_cast<signed char>(mVal); }
       39 +    explicit operator unsigned long int() const noexcept { return static_cast<unsigned long int>(mVal); }
       40 +    explicit operator unsigned int()      const noexcept { return static_cast<unsigned int>(mVal); }
       41 +    explicit operator bool()              const noexcept { return mVal != 0; }
       42 +
       43 +    // --- unary arithmetic ---------------------------------------------------
       44 +    MPint  operator+()         const noexcept { return *this; }
       45 +    MPint  operator-()         const noexcept { return MPint(-mVal); }
       46 +
       47 +    // --- unary logical ------------------------------------------------------
       48 +    bool   operator!()         const noexcept { return mVal == 0; }
       49 +
       50 +    // --- unary bitwise ------------------------------------------------------
       51 +    MPint  operator~()         const noexcept { return MPint(~mVal); }
       52 +
       53 +    // --- prefix increment / decrement ---------------------------------------
       54 +    MPint& operator++()    noexcept { ++mVal; return *this; }
       55 +    MPint& operator--()    noexcept { --mVal; return *this; }
       56 +
       57 +    // --- postfix increment / decrement --------------------------------------
       58 +    MPint  operator++(int) noexcept { MPint tmp(*this); ++mVal; return tmp; }
       59 +    MPint  operator--(int) noexcept { MPint tmp(*this); --mVal; return tmp; }
       60 +
       61 +    // --- compound assignment: arithmetic ------------------------------------
       62 +    MPint& operator+=(const MPint& rhs) noexcept { mVal += rhs.mVal; return *this; }
       63 +    MPint& operator-=(const MPint& rhs) noexcept { mVal -= rhs.mVal; return *this; }
       64 +    MPint& operator*=(const MPint& rhs) noexcept { mVal *= rhs.mVal; return *this; }
       65 +    MPint& operator/=(const MPint& rhs)          { mVal /= rhs.mVal; return *this; }
       66 +    MPint& operator%=(const MPint& rhs)          { mVal %= rhs.mVal; return *this; }
       67 +
       68 +    // --- compound assignment: bitwise ---------------------------------------
       69 +    MPint& operator&=(const MPint& rhs) noexcept { mVal &= rhs.mVal; return *this; }
       70 +    MPint& operator|=(const MPint& rhs) noexcept { mVal |= rhs.mVal; return *this; }
       71 +    MPint& operator^=(const MPint& rhs) noexcept { mVal ^= rhs.mVal; return *this; }
       72 +
       73 +    // --- compound assignment: shift -----------------------------------------
       74 +    MPint& operator<<=(int n) noexcept { mVal <<= n; return *this; }
       75 +    MPint& operator>>=(int n) noexcept { mVal >>= n; return *this; }
       76 +
       77 +    // --- binary arithmetic (defined via compound assignment) ----------------
       78 +    friend MPint operator+(MPint lhs, const MPint& rhs) noexcept { return lhs += rhs; }
       79 +    friend MPint operator-(MPint lhs, const MPint& rhs) noexcept { return lhs -= rhs; }
       80 +    friend MPint operator*(MPint lhs, const MPint& rhs) noexcept { return lhs *= rhs; }
       81 +    friend MPint operator/(MPint lhs, const MPint& rhs)          { return lhs /= rhs; }
       82 +    friend MPint operator%(MPint lhs, const MPint& rhs)          { return lhs %= rhs; }
       83 +
       84 +    // --- binary bitwise -----------------------------------------------------
       85 +    friend MPint operator&(MPint lhs, const MPint& rhs) noexcept { return lhs &= rhs; }
       86 +    friend MPint operator|(MPint lhs, const MPint& rhs) noexcept { return lhs |= rhs; }
       87 +    friend MPint operator^(MPint lhs, const MPint& rhs) noexcept { return lhs ^= rhs; }
       88 +
       89 +    // --- shift --------------------------------------------------------------
       90 +    friend MPint operator<<(MPint lhs, int n) noexcept { return lhs <<= n; }
       91 +    friend MPint operator>>(MPint lhs, int n) noexcept { return lhs >>= n; }
       92 +
       93 +    // --- comparison ---------------------------------------------------------
       94 +    friend bool operator==(const MPint& a, const MPint& b) noexcept { return a.mVal == b.mVal; }
       95 +    friend bool operator!=(const MPint& a, const MPint& b) noexcept { return a.mVal != b.mVal; }
       96 +    friend bool operator< (const MPint& a, const MPint& b) noexcept { return a.mVal <  b.mVal; }
       97 +    friend bool operator> (const MPint& a, const MPint& b) noexcept { return a.mVal >  b.mVal; }
       98 +    friend bool operator<=(const MPint& a, const MPint& b) noexcept { return a.mVal <= b.mVal; }
       99 +    friend bool operator>=(const MPint& a, const MPint& b) noexcept { return a.mVal >= b.mVal; }
      100 +
      101 +    // --- stream I/O ---------------------------------------------------------
      102 +    friend std::ostream& operator<<(std::ostream& os, const MPint& v);
      103 +    friend std::istream& operator>>(std::istream& is,       MPint& v);
      104 +
      105  private:
        8 -  long int mVal;
      106 +    signed long int mVal;
      107  };
      108 +
      109 +// Stream definitions live in MPint.cpp to avoid pulling <iostream> into every
      110 +// translation unit that includes this header.

● Builds clean (zero warnings with -Wall -Wextra -pedantic) and all checks pass. Here's what was done:

  src/MPint.hpp — the class now covers everything a signed long int can do:
  - Constructors from all common integral types (signed and unsigned)
  - explicit conversion operators back to primitive types (prevents silent truncation, but if (n) and bool contexts still work)
  - Unary: +, -, ~, !
  - Prefix/postfix ++ and --
  - Binary arithmetic: +, -, *, /, %
  - Binary bitwise: &, |, ^, <<, >>
  - All compound-assignment forms (+=, -=, *=, /=, %=, &=, |=, ^=, <<=, >>=)
  - All six comparisons
  - Stream <</>> declared as friend (defined in the .cpp to avoid pulling <iostream> into every TU that includes the header)

  src/MPint.cpp — only the stream operators, keeping the header lightweight.

  Makefile — make builds bin/demo; make clean removes it.

  src/demo.cpp — exercises every operator group and doubles as a smoke test.

  The mVal member is still signed long int, so swapping it for a GMP/PARI type later is purely a matter of changing that one line and adjusting the operator bodies.

      255 +  MPint a = 2, b = 3, c = 4;
      256 +  std::cout << "2 + 3 + 4     = " << (a + b + c)     << std::endl;  // 9

      258 +  std::cout << "2 + 3 * 4     = " << (a + b * c)     << std::endl;  // 14
      259 +  std::cout << "(2+3) * 4     = " << ((a + b) * c)   << std::endl;  // 20
      260 +  std::cout << "2 * 3 * 4     = " << (a * b * c)     << std::endl;  // 24
      261 +  MPint chain = 1;
      262 +  chain += 2;

      264 +  chain -= 1;

      268 +  MPint z1 = 0, z2 = 0;
      269 +  std::cout << "0 == 0 :: " << (z1 == z2) << std::endl;  // 1
      270 +  std::cout << "0 != 0 :: " << (z1 != z2) << std::endl;  // 0
      271 +  std::cout << "0 <  0 :: " << (z1 <  z2) << std::endl;  // 0
      272 +  std::cout << "0 <= 0 :: " << (z1 <= z2) << std::endl;  // 1
      273 +  std::cout << "0 >  0 :: " << (z1 >  z2) << std::endl;  // 0
      274 +  std::cout << "0 >= 0 :: " << (z1 >= z2) << std::endl;  // 1
      275 +  MPint neg = -1, pos = 1;
      276 +  std::cout << "-1 < 1  :: " << (neg < pos)  << std::endl;  // 1
      277 +  std::cout << "-1 > 1  :: " << (neg > pos)  << std::endl;  // 0
      278 +  std::cout << "-1 == 1 :: " << (neg == pos) << std::endl;  // 0
      279 +  std::cout << "-1 != 1 :: " << (neg != pos) << std::endl;  // 1
      280 +
      281 +  std::cout << std::endl << "--- bool / logical contexts ---" << std::endl;
      282 +  MPint t = 1, f = 0;
      283 +  std::cout << "bool(1)  = " << (t ? "true" : "false") << std::endl;   // true
      284 +  std::cout << "bool(0)  = " << (f ? "true" : "false") << std::endl;   // false
      285 +  std::cout << "!1       = " << !t << std::endl;  // 0
      286 +  std::cout << "!0       = " << !f << std::endl;  // 1
      287 +  std::cout << "!!42     = " << !!MPint(42) << std::endl;  // 1
      288 +

      292 +  std::cout << "-(-77)   = " << -(-p)   << std::endl;   // 77
      293 +  std::cout << "~~77     = " << ~~p     << std::endl;   // 77
      294 +  std::cout << "+(-77)   = " << +(-p)   << std::endl;   // -77
      295 +
      296 +  std::cout << std::endl << "--- self-operations ---" << std::endl;
      297 +  MPint s = 10;

      303 +  s += s;
      304 +  std::cout << "s += s (10) :: " << s   << std::endl;  // 20
      305 +

❯ I find it cumbersome to manually copy a session transcript into a text file. Yet I would like to have such a transcript, prefereably only one that is continued when a session ends. What do you have to offer?

● claude-code-guide(Claude Code session transcript options)
  ⎿  Done (4 tool uses · 29.0k tokens · 21s)
  (ctrl+o to expand)

● Honestly, Claude Code doesn't have a built-in "save transcript to file" feature. Here's what's actually available:

  What exists natively:
  - claude --continue / claude --resume <id> — continues a previous session with full context restored inside Claude Code, but doesn't export anything
  - Sessions are saved internally (you saw the resume ID at the end of session 03)
  - --output-format json|text — but only works with non-interactive claude -p "prompt" mode, not interactive sessions

  Practical options for you:

  1. Use script (the Unix command) — simplest, captures everything:
  script -a mpint-transcript.txt claude
  1. This appends the full terminal session (including your input and Claude's output) to a single file, every time. The -a flag appends rather than overwrites.
  2. Use --resume instead of transcripts — if the goal is continuity rather than a written record:
  claude --resume <session-id>
  2. Combined with my auto-memory, this may make transcripts unnecessary.
  3. My auto-memory — I now persist key context automatically, so even fresh sessions don't start from zero.

  The script command is probably the lowest-friction answer to what you're after — one running file, no manual copying. Want to try that approach?

❯ I'll go with 'script -a' _next_time_ i start claude. 'CLAUDE.md' also is under git control, so you stay informed across sessions, if in doubt (_if_ ever you are 'in doubt' sometimes :)). In the future me might work on several
  unrelated projects, in different directories. Will you be able to keep your session memory in the respective context?

● Yes. The auto-memory is per-project directory. The current one lives at:

  ~/.claude/projects/-home-claudeuser-work-mpint/memory/

  If you start Claude Code in, say, ~/work/otherthing/, it would use a separate memory directory:

  ~/.claude/projects/-home-claudeuser-work-otherthing/memory/

  So each project gets its own isolated memory — no cross-contamination. The CLAUDE.md in each project root works the same way, scoped to that directory.

  The combination of project-scoped CLAUDE.md (you control) + project-scoped auto-memory (I maintain) should keep things tidy across multiple projects.

❯ After having that ('CAUDE.md', auto-memory, transcript) put to work according to your recommendations, I wonder whether there is any additional advantage left in using 'claude --continue' or 'claude --resume <id>'.

● Good question. Yes, there's one thing those three don't replicate:

  --continue/--resume restores the full conversational context — every message, every tool call, every intermediate reasoning step from the previous session. It's as if the session never ended.

  With the other three mechanisms, a new session starts with:
  - CLAUDE.md — your requirements (compact, curated)
  - Auto-memory — my notes (compact, curated)
  - Transcript — full record, but I only read it if you ask me to (and it's raw terminal output with script -a, not the cleanest format)

  The difference matters when you're mid-task. If you're halfway through a complex refactoring, close the terminal, and come back — --resume picks up exactly where you left off. With a fresh session, I'd need to re-orient, re-read files,
  and reconstruct what I was doing.

  In practice:
  - --resume is valuable for interrupted work — same day, same task, pick up where you stopped
  - For next-day / new-task sessions, CLAUDE.md + auto-memory is sufficient and cleaner (no stale context from yesterday's half-finished thoughts)

  So: use --resume when you got interrupted, start fresh when you're moving on to new work. The three mechanisms you have cover the fresh-start case well.

❯ Whats the difference between '--resume' and '--continue'?

● --continue resumes the most recent session automatically.

  --resume <id> resumes a specific session by its ID.

  That's the only difference. Same mechanism, just --continue is the shorthand when you want "whatever I was doing last."

● See you next time. The auto-memory is set, CLAUDE.md is under git, and you'll have script -a for transcripts. You're all set.
